class=\"exercise\"><h2>Reading Practice</h2><p class=\"meta\">先完成选择，再查看解析。练习内容不计入词汇正文覆盖。</p><p class=\"meta\" data-score=\"95\"></p><button type=\"button\" data-reset=\"95\">清空本章作答，重新自测</button><div class=\"question\" id=\"q-96-1\" data-question=\"95,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. 推断</p><p><strong>What do Mara&#x27;s wedding promises suggest about her understanding of commitment?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. Independence is a temporary difficulty that marriage will remove.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. A committed couple should never have a disagreement.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. Love makes explicit conversation unnecessary.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. A lasting relationship involves returning to difficult conversations and respecting each other&#x27;s choices.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"94\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"96\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x001c01","title":"The Invitation That Ate the Honeymoon","volume":"婚后外传：误入博物馆的蜜月","anchor":"chapter-0097","paragraphs":["The museum curator met them at the station with flowers for Mara and a live snail for Cassian. The flowers were a wedding gift. The snail, he explained, was evidence.","\"You might have put that on the envelope,\" Cassian said.","\"I thought it would daunt you.\"","\"A useful precaution,\" Mara said, taking the little ventilated box. \"We are already extensively married. There is no reason to make the journey respectable.\"","The curator, Dr. Felix Dene, looked relieved. He was a conscientious man with a fatherly manner and a habit of polishing objects that were already adequately clean. His museum was funded by the town and a small trust. Neither entity had infinite resources. If a scandal frightened its donors away, he feared they would have nothing left for the winter program.","They walked up a bleak little hill to an house that looked exceptionally prosperous. The museum had once belonged to a cloth merchant who had wanted overseas visitors to mistake him for a prince. There were stone lions at the door, classical-music recordings in the hall, and a fountain whose mechanism made a noise like an angry beast. Green moss had gathered beside its overflow, where water constantly dampened the stone. A postcard of Blenheim Palace stood on the reception desk, apparently to remind visitors that grandeur elsewhere might involve fewer plumbing difficulties.","Felix showed them the missing painting's replacement. It depicted a boy offering a bowl to a strange, scaled creature. One corner bore a grey-green stain. The snail had been found on the frame, although the gallery had no windows that opened onto the garden. Beside it stood a small copper artefact, taken from a different room. The quantity of dust beneath its base suggested it had been moved recently.","\"The stain was known as the painter's curse,\" Felix said. \"A colorful description, albeit not a scientific one. A visiting expert called it a phenomenon inherent in his technique.\"","\"A substance can have a history without a curse,\" Mara said. \"Has a conservator examined it?\"","A team of analysts had taken a sample. Their report was due to reach the museum that afternoon. As yet, Felix had received only an ambiguous message: ORGANISM PRESENT. He had interpreted this as a warning that the painting might be at high risk of decay. He had consequently closed the entire gallery.","Mara looked at the snail. \"You cannot bank on a message that doesn't tell you which sample it describes.\"","Cassian's gaze moved from the frame to the fresh polish on its bracket. Someone had begun cleaning the wall, then stopped. Mara watched Felix begin checking the other frames. She was trying to establish whether the disturbed dust belonged to one event or several. He asked whether the replacement had been subjected to the same inspection as the other exhibits. Felix hesitated.","\"It was meant to center on the same artist,\" he said. \"The visiting expert provided the desired attribution.\"","\"An attribution is not an inspection,\" Mara said gently.","The expert's visit had coincided with preparations for a new fellowship. Its sponsors wanted the museum to evolve to become an education center, with interactivity replacing silent rooms. The plan was broadly attractive. Yet the fellowship selected students almost entirely by their grade-point average, and the proposed entrance test contained elaborate linguistic tasks unrelated to restoration. Applicants who were fluent in the sponsors' preferred language would have an advantage even if they could not safely lift a frame.","\"It is advertised as open to all-comers,\" Felix said. \"The selection committee insists it does not play favorites.\"","\"Then let it show how it chooses,\" Mara said. \"An inherent advantage can be real even when nobody has consciously invented it.\"","Felix looked towards a girl sitting beside the reception desk. Pippa, nineteen, had restored the fountain's broken model lion. She had been born with a small scar beside one eyebrow, which deepened when she frowned at a difficult mechanism. She was now frowning at an envelope.","Her application had been rejected. The fellowship was state-sponsored, but its public purpose had somehow narrowed to funding people who already resembled the committee. Pippa's workshop experience was extensive; her mathematical examination score was ordinary. Mara could hardly ignore the distinction. A fair-minded committee might reject Pippa for a relevant weakness; it could not reasonably hide the criterion. Mara could not yet say the decision was wrong. She could say the reasons cry out for a better explanation.","\"That may come across as adversarial,\" Felix warned.","\"Only if questions are treated as attacks.\"","In the wake of the rejection, Pippa had continued repairing the fountain, unwilling to let a committee decide whether she should enjoy her work. She looked up. \"I don't want to assassinate anyone's reputation. I want to know why the advertised rules weren't applicable to my application.\"","Felix blushed. He had signed the rejection himself, trusting the committee's summary. An erroneous conclusion could be sincerely held and still have disastrous consequences. His motivation had been to protect the museum. His motive in withholding the letter from Mara was less flattering: he had hoped an emerging problem would disappear before she noticed it.","Before he could answer Pippa, a well-dressed man entered carrying the confidence of someone who had never waited beside a reception desk. Sebastian Fleet was the expert who had authenticated the replacement. He gave Mara an admiring smile that did not quite conceal annoyance.","\"I advise you to enjoy the honeymoon,\" he said. \"Never mind the administrative detail.\"","\"I married a detail enthusiast,\" Mara said. \"It is a lifelong difficulty.\"","Fleet laughed, then said the museum could contract out of several obligations if the trust changed its status. He mentioned anti-avoidance rules as if naming them were evidence that his arrangements complied with them. The museum's accountant would check the proposal, he said. He wanted to renew the town's business through workshops that would industrialize old crafts. Mara wondered what legislative oversight the state-sponsored scheme required. Mara asked to see that proposal. He said it was not available.","\"You miss the point if you imagine a small museum can afford unlimited openness.\"","\"And you miss the point if you imagine limited funds make secrecy free.\"","Cassian took her hand before the exchange became needlessly sharp. The contact was not a restraint. It reminded her that they had come to solve a missing painting, not to enjoy every available argument.","The gallery's visitor record had also been corrupted by a failed import, leaving the timing uncertain. Felix was due to explain the gap at the afternoon meeting. Cassian asked what the new trust would stand for if its old promises proved unrealistic. Fleet ignored him. The analyst's report arrived while Fleet was still explaining why questions about his company would rob the town of investment. The organism was a harmless garden snail. The message had referred to a photograph of the shell, not to mold in the paint. Closing the gallery had been prudent until the uncertainty was resolved; continuing to cite the message after clarification would not be prudent at all.","As a result, Felix reopened the room. Visitors began entering from the courtyard. Fleet became oddly dizzy and sat down. Mara watched his eyes move to the fountain, where Pippa had begun adjusting the water flow. She worked joyfully when nobody interrupted her. Her aural attention was excellent: she could hear when the model pump was working too hard.","\"The long-run costs of your project will vary with maintenance,\" Mara said to Fleet. \"I would like Pippa to team up with the person preparing those estimates.\"","\"We have a candidate who will get the nod tomorrow,\" he replied.","\"On what evidence?\"","\"Don't defy me simply because you enjoy an audience.\"","At that moment a man in a delivery coat lifted the alleged original's empty packing case. A dark rolled canvas slipped from his sleeve. Pippa saw it first. She cried out; the man ran.","Mara set off after him before anyone could homogenize the moment into a committee procedure. She caught the courtyard rail, swung down three steps, and landed ahead of the cart blocking the gate. Cassian came around its other side. The delivery man dropped the roll and climbed over the low wall.","Pippa caught the canvas. It was blank. The chase had drawn all three of them away from the gallery.","Inside, the fountain stopped. A door clicked. Fleet was gone, and the replacement painting with him.","Mara looked at her husband's muddy shoes. \"You said the museum would be quiet.\"","\"You said it. I was admirably skeptical.\"","Behind them, Pippa began laughing. They had not overcome the difficulty, but at least they had finally found its shape. The town's supposedly pure cultural gift contained an illusory painting, an illusory competition, and a man who ran when asked to explain either. In the face of a confident liar and a closing door, Mara found it impossible to remain an ordinary tourist. The decision was theirs to investigate now; she meant to make it carefully."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1506,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0097\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div museum's accountant, and a former schoolteacher who knew where every delivery driver in town ate supper. Only the schoolteacher had produced an immediately useful address.","Fleet had rented a room above the Golden Hearth. He was due to leave town at dawn. The innkeeper remembered him as a remarkably fluent guest who constantly requested another key, another towel, another exception. She had not been adequately paid for the first room and was delighted to hear that he might be subjected to questioning.","\"He came across as a gentleman,\" she told Cassian. \"But broadly, I've had better experiences with plumbers.\"","Mara tried to look solemn. The ambiguous phrase \"collection services\" appeared twice in the inn's booking record: once beside a parcel and once beside the suitcase. The label concealed two distinct transactions. Cassian failed. The innkeeper showed them an empty bedroom, where a suitcase lay open beside a classical-music magazine. A postcard of Blenheim Palace had been tucked into a page about sponsorship. Across its back were three figures and the name of the museum trust.","Pippa read the figures over Mara's shoulder. She was not a mathematical genius, but she could subtract a fee from a grant. The remainder was substantially smaller than the fellowship publicity suggested. In the wake of the theft, the expensive project began to look less generous than the old workshop it would replace.","\"Perhaps there are legitimate costs,\" Cassian said. \"We don't know the accounts.\"","\"We know what the promises cry out for,\" Mara replied. \"An accountant.\"","The accountant arrived carrying a brown bag and an adversarial relationship with unnecessary expenditure. Her name was Orla. She had spent the afternoon checking which legislative requirements were applicable to the trust. Fleet had claimed that the new company could contract out of its obligations to the museum simply by taking the work overseas. Orla doubted the claim and had sent it to an independent adviser.","\"He has also called my anti-avoidance questions personal hostility,\" she said. \"They are arithmetic questions. He is welcome to answer them with numbers.\"","They were not going to assassinate his character, Orla insisted. An erroneous allegation would hurt the museum and help him. The inquiry must center on the missing funds and the terms actually agreed. Their desired result was recovery of the painting and a funded fellowship whose money reached its students.","On the bed, a sheet of publicity described Pippa as an unqualified local girl attempting to defy expert judgment. Someone had begun writing a public explanation for rejecting her before the final application had been assessed. The statement did not merely simplify the evidence; it replaced it.","Pippa's face lost its color. \"They wanted to make me the obstacle.\"","\"They wanted to homogenize the applicants,\" Mara said. \"Your experience made the comparison inconvenient.\"","Orla knew the selection figures. She wanted to bank on a fair process, not a generous gesture that might later be withdrawn. As yet, the promised review had no agreed date. The committee had used grade-point average as though every school awarded marks on the same basis. A linguistic test had then excluded candidates whose training was practical. Calling the competition open to all-comers did not overcome those differences. Nor did it prove that every rejected applicant should get the nod. A fair-minded review would need to look at the work.","\"I don't want them to play favorites for me now,\" Pippa said.","\"Then we have something on which to bank,\" Cassian replied.","The innkeeper returned with another key. Fleet had paid for a second room, booked under the name of the trust rather than his own. Treating the trust as a separate entity had apparently made it easier for him to charge the first room twice. The distinction was real; his use of it was not automatically honest.","The second door would not open. From inside came the scrape of a chair. Cassian knocked and identified himself. Someone shouted that the window was a more attractive exit. In town, the Golden Hearth was known as the safest respectable inn. Fleet seemed determined to test the limits of both adjectives.","\"That sounds like him,\" Mara said.","She was already at the corridor's far end. A small window opened above a covered passage. The roof below it sloped towards Fleet's room. This was at high risk of becoming an exceptionally bad idea, but the alternative was waiting while he destroyed evidence. She asked the innkeeper whether the roof would support her weight. A dangerous climb would daunt her less than an unrealistic assumption about the tiles. She could hardly pretend the consequences belonged only to her.","\"It supported my husband last summer.\"","\"A useful comparison.\"","Cassian looked at the window. \"I'll go first.\"","\"You're taller, heavier, and wearing the expensive coat.\"","\"I thought we agreed you liked the coat.\"","\"I intend to renew my acquaintance with it later. Hold the window.\"","The tiles were wet. Moss covered the low wall beneath the roof, making the narrow landing unsuitable for a jump. Mara moved slowly, trying to keep her feet above the wooden supports. This was not pure bravery. It was a prudent response to limited choices, and she was frightened enough to be conscientious about every step.","Below, Pippa watched an officer begin moving the street barriers away from the ladder. Fleet appeared at the next window with the painting rolled beneath his arm. He stared at her as though the laws of architecture had betrayed him.","\"Go back,\" he said. \"You'll fall.\"","\"Then stop making me come this way.\"","When Mara was due to cross the second slope, a violin sounded from the street. Pippa had borrowed an instrument from a performer outside the inn, and was playing a tune the museum used in its aural identification game. Felix, following the music, looked up and immediately began arguing with the innkeeper about ladders. Interactivity, Mara thought, had developed an unexpected practical purpose.","Fleet tried to move along the tiles. His suitcase caught on the frame. He yanked it free, lost his balance, and grabbed the gutter. The painting slid towards the edge. Its tie set off a little cascade of loose dirt. Mara caught its loose tie. She was dizzy now, though she kept her gaze on the frame rather than the ground.","\"The support beside your hand is rotten,\" she said. \"Come back towards me.\"","\"You want the painting.\"","\"I also want you to remain mortal in the ordinary, unhurried sense. Move.\"","He hesitated. Whatever his inherent weaknesses, a desire to die for a fraudulent fellowship was not among them. He moved. Cassian reached through the window and caught his collar. Pippa held the other end of the ladder below. By the time the police arrived, Fleet was sitting on a chair, albeit not the chair he had chosen, and Mara was enclosed in the coat she had promised to admire later.","Orla opened the suitcase in front of the officers. The folder inside contained altered invoices and a corrupted copy of the selection record. A second memory device held an earlier version. Analysts would need to compare them; a file's existence did not establish who had changed it. Still, as a result of preserving both versions, an emerging accusation could now evolve to become a testable case.","Mara unrolled the canvas. The painted boy and creature had returned, but the lower edge showed a fresh tear. The substance that appeared to be old discoloration was a recent coating, applied to make the replacement look convincingly neglected. The snail had crossed it before being accidentally boxed with the picture. An organism had supplied a clue where an expert had supplied a story. Orla called the episode a familiar phenomenon: impressive credentials discouraging an ordinary question. The small copper artefact, found in the second room, had been used to hold the rolled canvas flat.","Felix looked ill. His fatherly encouragement of Fleet had seemed harmless. He had trusted a man because the man reminded him of the curator he once hoped to become. The damage was real, though his original motivation had been kind.","\"Don't let shame rob us of the next useful hour,\" Mara said. \"Never mind the speech you think you owe us. Let us get started on the record.\"","They would team up with the regional conservation workshop, consult its technicians extensively, and downscale the proposed opening until the accounts were clear. The state-sponsored grant might be suspended. That would be bleak, but pretending the long-run costs were known would be disastrous.","Pippa began folding the publicity sheets so their blank sides could be used for notes. Mara watched her work joyfully rather than angrily. She had been born with the little scar. Her patience, on the other hand, had been learned.","\"Would an apology help?\" Felix asked her.","\"It would stand for something,\" Pippa said. \"If the rules change too.\"","Cassian tucked Mara's cold hand into his pocket. \"Tomorrow we have nothing left to investigate,\" he murmured.","She glanced at Orla's expanding pile of invoices. \"That seems an illusory prospect.\"","\"I live on emerging hope.\"","In the face of the innkeeper's bill for the damaged gutter, hope was a useful resource. Orla reminded Felix that they would miss the point if they treated the bill as the main loss. It could vary without warning, but at least it had brought them both back through the same window."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1574,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0098\" data-go=\"98\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x001c03","title":"The Prize Nobody Could Buy","volume":"婚后外传：误入博物馆的蜜月","anchor":"chapter-0099","paragraphs":["As a result of the independent review, the fellowship reopened three weeks later, albeit on a smaller budget and with a remarkably large audience. Felix had expected the scandal to daunt every applicant. He had been constantly checking the empty display case, as though another look could change its contents. Instead, the workshop received twice as many requests for information. People who had dismissed the old promise as illusory wanted to know whether the new one had substance.","The revised competition was open to all-comers. Pippa was due to demonstrate the fountain that afternoon. For once she felt pleasantly dizzy with anticipation, rather than anger. Its first task involved a damaged chair, a loose frame, and an artefact whose purpose was deliberately ambiguous. Candidates had to say what they knew, what they could test, and what they should leave to someone better qualified. A fluent explanation earned no extra credit if the proposed treatment would destroy the object.","Orla observed from the doorway. She had been adequately paid for her work and intended to remain that way. The trust could not contract out of obligations simply by transferring invoices to an overseas entity, the independent review had concluded. Which anti-avoidance provisions applied to its financial arrangements was still being examined; nobody advertised uncertainty as a victory.","Fleet was being investigated over the painting and the altered accounts. He was not present to offer a fatherly interpretation of the rules. His lawyer said the allegations would be contested. Mara kept the museum's statement factual. It did not need to assassinate him to explain why its procedures had changed.","\"A less adversarial tone than I expected,\" Cassian observed.","\"You weren't here when Orla revised the catering budget.\"","Orla raised an eyebrow. \"The desired result was lunch. Lunch remains funded.\"","Outside, the bleak hill had turned green. Pippa was showing a class how a small organism could leave marks on a surface without becoming responsible for every mark found there. The snail had retired to the garden. The children had named it Professor Fleet, a development Felix was trying to discourage without appearing needlessly authoritarian.","\"The joke could evolve to become cruelty,\" he told them. \"The snail has done nothing dishonest.\"","Mara laughed. Felix no longer sounded like a man rehearsing the opinions of a more impressive person. He could be conscientious without pretending to know everything. His original motivation had survived an unflattering examination of its effects.","The selection committee had changed as well. Its members now had to identify which criterion was applicable to each task. Grade-point average was retained as one piece of information, not a universal measure of aptitude. A linguistic assessment remained for work requiring translation; it could not quietly exclude a candidate from repairing wood. Scores could vary, and reasons for a decision had to be recorded.","\"That won't overcome every unfairness,\" Pippa said.","\"No,\" Mara agreed. \"But it may stop a convenient number from making all the decisions.\"","Its older visitor files remained corrupted. Felix had begun a clean register, which would be subjected to routine checks rather than treated as infallible. The new ledger was known as the Book of Ordinary Questions, a title chosen by the children. The museum's new interactivity did not center on expensive screens. One room invited visitors to compare a replica with a repaired original. Another offered an aural exercise: listen to three recorded mechanical sounds and decide which observation you would need before diagnosing the fault. The instructions warned that confidence was not evidence. Felix had written them himself.","Mara tested the exhibit with Cassian. He gave an erroneous answer to the first question and an elaborately defended erroneous answer to the second. When she asked why, he said he had hoped to get the nod through charm.","\"We don't play favorites,\" she told him.","\"A disastrous policy for our household.\"","\"Never mind the household. Try to answer the question.\"","He did, and on the third attempt heard the distinction. \"We miss the point when we only try to sound certain,\" he admitted. His aural attention was improving. He kissed her cheek in celebration, which the instructions had failed to forbid.","At noon, Pippa entered the examination room. The unusual object was a small wooden beast whose mouth had jammed. Mara saw her begin testing the loose joint with her fingers. She did not begin forcing the mouth open. She asked whether the committee wanted the mechanism repaired or simply the cause identified. That question, the assessors later explained, substantially changed their understanding of her work.","Other candidates impressed them too. One had a mathematical approach that clarified the movement. Another knew how to document materials. The prize did not go to Pippa alone. Three paid places replaced the single, grandly advertised award. It was prudent to downscale the publicity and expand the actual opportunity.","Pippa would team up with an older apprentice who had spent years repairing agricultural machinery. Neither was born with the knowledge the other possessed. They would consult the workshop's analysts where tests were needed and learn to recognize the limits of their own methods. The museum would not try to homogenize them into identical experts.","As a result, the celebration became crowded. A team of musicians offered a short classical-music performance in the hall. The fountain started making its beastly noise halfway through the first piece. Pippa listened, adjusted a valve, and returned to her seat. Nobody described the intervention as a miracle. The children applauded anyway. Pure satisfaction crossed Pippa's face. The pressure on the valve had set off a fault elsewhere; the small correction had stopped it.","A donor, returning in the wake of the scandal, asked whether the original scheme could be renewed without mentioning the missing money. His company wanted to industrialize the town's old workshops and use the museum's reputation in its campaign. Orla said the grant could be renewed; the public record would remain.","\"You make us come across as suspicious merely for asking,\" he complained.","\"Questions cry out for answers,\" she said. \"If you have good answers, this is an opportunity.\"","The donor considered leaving. Then he looked at the room full of families, counted his alternatives, and stayed. Mara could not bank on his generosity lasting forever. The point was to make the museum less dependent on any single person's goodwill.","By afternoon, Felix was speak
